===== [p156-156] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > H.1 Newly developed alignment concept by TNO (4块) =====
--[p156]-- If a camera would look directly at the beam splitter, the image plane of the of the optics would be tilted with respect to the die and substrate. This would result in a out of focus image of the die on the sensor. The optical design to make the co‐location concept successful was developed by TNO.
--[p156]-- 9‐57), the image of both the die and the substrate are projected onto a single camera either simultaneously or subsequently. The images of both die and substrate are projected on the image processor by means of a beam splitter. After image readout and processing, the number of pixels of the image sensor between identified reference marks on both die and substrate is a measure of their actual offset.
The actual realization of the optical design is less straight forward.

--[p156]-- H.1 Newly developed alignment concept by TNO

===== [p208-208] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.1 Introduction (2块) =====
--[p208]-- L.1 Introduction
The clamping force of an electrostatic clamp should be evaluated to assess its potential in the application of an electrostatic tape to transport dies. The aim is a relatively cheap test setup with minimal parts, which is easy to realize. The chosen materials should represent feasible materials used in an electrostatic tape.
--[p208]-- The idea of the experiment is more to get a general idea of the clamping force that can be realized and the limitations of the clamp and less to get a fully accurate measurement of this clamping force. The reason for this is that fully accurate measurements currently hold only limited additional value over simple measurements and would require much more effort than is possible in the available time span.

===== [p29-29] Department of Precision and Microsystems Engineering > 2.4 Flexibility (4块) =====
--[p29]-- For example, the bumps on the dies can differ in dimensions, pattern, location and material. It is preferred to have a concept which does not limit the type of die to be stacked. The stack height of the 3D‐ICs will also vary in the number of layers, die thickness and standoff distance (vertical spacing between dies). This results in different stack heights. Total thicknesses of up to 1 [mm] are currently expected.
--[p29]-- 2.4 Flexibility
3D‐IC being a new trend with a lot of research going on, there is a good possibility that the process flow and requirements will change in the future. In other words, the different consortia on 3D‐IC have not consolidated on ‘the best’ process to create 3D‐ICs.
--[p29]-- Some heavily researched areas are for example the creation of TSVs, bumping of TSVs, thinning of wafers and thin wafer handling, dicing of wafers and the techniques required to actually bond the dies mechanically and electrically together. The newly developed pick and place concept should limit the imposed restrictions on the other process steps as much as possible. Also, it is likely that the dies to be handled by the PnP machine vary in layout.
--[p29]-- This has for example implications on the alignment system, which will need the ability to adapt to changing stack heights (even between stacks on the same wafer because of die nonuniformity).

===== [p160-160] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > H.3.3.2 Nozzle to XY stage (2块) =====
--[p160]-- H.3.3.2 Nozzle to XY stage
This problem can be solved by using the nozzle and wafer table as references.
The easier implementation of the interferometer comes at a cost. The downside is that the measurement loop is extended, increasing the sensitivity to disturbances. The use of the wafer stage as reference also comes at a cost. This requires the sides of the wafer stage to be equipped with a mirror over the full stroke.
--[p160]-- Despite being expensive, calibration routines are also required to remove any imperfections in these mirrors, which is not impossible but is again an additional process. Besides this, since the nozzle is used as reference, the die position relative to the nozzle should be measured as well. Since it is likely that a vision system is required for this, some of the added accuracy value of the interferometer system is lost since final placement accuracy is never better than the camera resolution.

===== [p84-84] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 7.4 Conclusions and reflection on electrostatic flexible carrier (1块) =====
--[p84]-- 7.4 Conclusions and reflection on electrostatic flexible carrier
Despite the data manipulation required to get the presented results; it is concluded that the manufactured clamp works as expected. It was also verified the clamp can pick up and release dies and wafers with short clamping and declamping times. The required operating voltages of the developed clamp are much lower than what is common for current electrostatic wafer clamps.
Two reasons for this were the required holding force and the small air gap.

===== [p137-137] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > F.3 Single nozzle (1块) =====
--[p137]-- F.3 Single nozzle
The source wafer is placed on a wafer stage. This wafer stage positions a die on a defined location under the flip unit. The flip unit picks the die from the wafer and rotates the die, presenting it to a bond head placed on a gantry system. The bond head moves to the placement location and places the die on the target. This is a highly serial process which results as illustrated by the cycle time equation. The estimated minimal cycle time, excluding measurements etc.
is 600 [ms].
$$
t _ { _ { t o t } } = t _ { _ { p i c k } } + t _ { _ { f l i p } } + 2 \cdot t _ { _ { t r } } + t _ { _ { p r e s } }
$$

===== [p65-65] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 6.3.2 Electrostatic (5块) =====
--[p65]-- The multipolar clamp is an extension of the bipolar clamp, having more than two oppositely charged plates but working by the same principle as the bipolar clamp.
The most likely setup is the lower one, the bipolar clamp, since it is not desirable to have the need for an electrical connection to the die itself. This is best avoided because the electrical connection takes time to establish and disconnect.
--[p65]-- In the scope of an electrostatic clamp, the electrons in a conductor or dielectric are attracted by the electric field, creating the holding force between object and electrode. Although this seems like an exotic solution, electrostatic wafer clamps are already used in some front‐end semiconductor applications. An electrostatic carrier based on a silicon wafer is shown in Fig. 6‐3. Some more examples are found in [6] [23] [24].
--[p65]-- In electrostatic clamp design a clear distinction can be made between two different designs, shown in Fig. 6‐4. The upper is a monopolar clamp and the lower is a bipolar or multipolar clamp. The monopolar clamp has an electrical connection to the charged plate as well as to the object to be clamped. The bipolar clamp has two oppositely charged plates and requires no electrical connection to the object to be clamped.
--[p65]-- Also, the die is charged with the risk of residual charges remaining on the die after removal. The use of electrostatics in combination with sensitive electronic circuits is not trivial. Usually measures are taken to avoid charging electronic circuits, one reason for this is the risk of electrostatic discharge (ESD). A summary of the advantages and disadvantages of electrostatic clamps is stated below.
--[p65]-- 6.3.2 Electrostatic
Electrostatic adhesion is based on the physical principle that an electric field attracts or repels electric charge. The formula describing this phenomenon is called ‘Coulomb’s Law’. The formula below shows the simplified form.
$$
\overrightarrow { F _ { A B } } = q _ { A } \cdot \overrightarrow { E _ { B } }
$$
This formula shows that the electrostatic force is proportional to the electric field (E) and the charge (q) on which a force is applied by this electric field.

===== [p39-39] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 3.3.1.2 General conclusions and alternate solutions General conclusions: (2块) =====
--[p39]-- 3.3.1.2 General conclusions and alternate solutions General conclusions:
 Startstop costs a lot of cycle time
o  try to avoid stopping
o  try to make one movement from source to target; dont split up XY and Z movements
 For minimized setup time, jerk must be limiting $( 3 ^ { \mathsf { r d } }$ order profiles)
 For jerk limiting profiles; acceleration is increased a lot with respect to stateoftheart solutions
--[p39]--  Increasing jerk over 10000 $[ \mathsf { m } / \mathsf { s } ^ { 3 } ]$ is (relatively) not very effective for reducing cycle times, when travelling 300 [mm]. Parallelization will be much more effective in this case
 5 dies/s with one robot is not realistic
Alternate solutions to decrease cycle time:
 Parallel endeffectors in bond head in one pick & place robot
 Parallel pick & place robots in one machine
 Parallel pick & place machines
 Reduce distance

===== [p32-32] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 3.1.2 Market segments (2块) =====
--[p32]-- 3.1.2 Market segments
When considering pick and place equipment different market segments can be distinguished with each their own performance specifications in terms of speed, accuracy and flexibility. First there is the high volume, low accuracy Surface Mount Technology (SMT) equipment. These machines are designed to place many different kinds of components on PCBs. Fig. 3‐1 shows for example a vacuum nozzle placing a component on a PCB.
--[p32]-- The components reach from packaged dies, small and large capacitors and coils to for example the USB connection ports on the motherboard in your home computer. The main players in this market are Assembleon, Fuji and Siemens. The accuracy and throughput reached by these machines are up to 121000 units per hour (UPH) and 40 [μm] (3σ) placement accuracy. Though the high placement accuracy is not necessarily reached at the highest throughput and also varies with the type of component placed.

===== [p109-109] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.1.1.1 Wafer table (1块) =====
--[p109]-- D.1.1.1 Wafer table
The dies for both gantries are supplied on a single wafer. The wafer table is located in the bottom of the pick and place system, as shown in Fig. 9‐15. A more detailed view of the wafer table assembly is shown in Fig. 9‐15.
The wafer table has the ability to move in the horizontal plane and rotate about the vertical axis. The function of the wafer table is to supply the flip units with chips by moving the diced wafer to the appropriate position under the flip units.

===== [p223-223] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.8.2.2 Insulator stiffness (1块) =====
--[p223]-- L.8.2.2 Insulator stiffness
As discussed in section L.5.1.1 and Fig. 9‐134, the compliance of the insulator material causes it to be compressed under load. The force required for this is supplied by the clamp itself. Therefore less force is available for the actual clamping of the sample.
$$
F _ { i n t e r f a c e } = F _ { c l a m p } - F _ { e l a s t }
$$

--[p38]-- 3.3.1.1 Limitations of motion profile parameters
Important reasons for the motion profile limitations in backend semiconductor equipment are stated below.
 Jerk limited by:
o Electronics  self inductance of motor windings and maximum output voltage of amplifier
 Acceleration limited by:
o Reaction forces and resulting disturbances  high accelerations require high mechanical eigenfrequencies to avoid excessive resulting disturbances

===== [p59-59] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 5.3.1.1 Opportunities (1块) =====
--[p59]-- 5.3.1.1 Opportunities
To improve the accuracy of this system further, the downward motion of the nozzle should have very high repeatability in XY or it should be corrected for. Three suggestions are:
 Using a compliant nozzle (high repeatability)
o Avoid slipping of die on substrate
o Avoid breaking loose of die from nozzle
 Use vision in the loop to measure nozzle or die motion during placement and correct for this
o To track die, good depth of field is preferred (small Numerical Aperture)
 Use accelerometers attached to nozzle or phi‐z actuator and to wafer stage to measure relative motion and correct for this

===== [p117-117] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.1.2.2 G0 matching of left and right gantry (1块) =====
--[p117]-- D.4.1.2.2 G0 matching of left and right gantry
Both gantries measure the location of the two G0 markers (G0 position marker, G0 angle marker) with the gantry scales while the substrate camera is focused on G0. From this the gantries are matched to the same G0 (main) co‐ordinate system. Important error sources are:
● Base calibration errors
 G0 marker accuracy
 Gantry scales
 SC calibration
 Vibrations (external, roller guide, reaction forces)

===== [p100-100] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > B.2 Throughput (1块) =====
--[p100]-- B.2 Throughput
Typical throughput for state‐of‐the‐art pick and place equipment is up to about 10000 components per hour for die bonders, as discussed in section 3.1. For of 3D integration of dies to be economically viable in the future, it is estimated that a placement speed of 5 dies/sec is needed. Even though this value resulted from an extensive business case analysis, this value can be illustrated with the a simplified cost of ownership (CoO) analysis as stated in Table 9‐1.

===== [p153-153] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.5 Induced vibrations (1块) =====
--[p153]-- G.2.5 Induced vibrations
Vibrations of for example the die relative to the target or the measurement equipment relative to the measured object can cause placement errors. These vibrations are often induced by moving parts inside the machine itself. Large masses moved with high accelerations cause large reaction forces and therefore often large frame vibrations. These vibration amplitudes can even exceed the allowed total placement error.
Measurements of vibration levels and their effect on placement accuracy are reported in Appendix M.

===== [p196-196] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > K.4 Johnson Rahbek forces (2块) =====
--[p196]-- 9‐105, instead of the electric charge running from for example the top capacitor plate of the bottom capacitor to the bottom plate of the top capacitor, the plates are charged due to imperfections in the insulating properties of the layers, allowing a small current to run through the capacitors.
--[p196]-- K.4 Johnson Rahbek forces
For the capacitive clamp it was assumed that the dielectric material was ideal and had infinite resistance. In contrast to the capacitive clamp theory, which is based on the capacitors being charged due to charge redistribution between the capacitors, the Johnson Rahbek (JR) forces are caused by current leakage over the dielectric layer. So in Fig.

===== [p57-57] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 5.1.2.3 Placing sequence (2块) =====
--[p57]-- The sequence for picking dies from the source wafer is of course very similar. It is possible to pickup only the known good dies, or to pickup all dies and test them during PnP.
--[p57]-- 5.1.2.3 Placing sequence
The assembly sequence on the target wafer is relatively straight forward. Each die is placed one position after the previous one to minimize the distance to be covered and therefore the required accelerations and corresponding reaction forces. Of course with exception of the locations on the target wafer where defect stacks or dies were detected, one of the benefits of the current concept over direct wafer‐2‐wafer bonding.

===== [p20-20] Department of Precision and Microsystems Engineering > 1.2.1 Flip chip (3块) =====
--[p20]-- This resulted in much more and shorter interconnects with less parasitic electrical properties. The flip chip assembly is until now only interesting for high performance applications because the decreased parasitic and high input / output (I/O) count are required here. For low end applications with limited IC architecture change the IC redesign cost is still a barrier to use flip chip [5]. Flip chip is becoming more common though.
--[p20]-- <sup>5</sup> Flip chip: The bare chip is turned over so that its electronic circuits face the substrate. After mounting the chip, the chips own silicon back side provides the protection otherwise provided by an additional package.
--[p20]-- 1.2.1 Flip chip
Traditionally wire bonding was used to connect single dies to their package. But the increasing number of required connections were difficult to fit on the perimeter alone. A solution was found in using the surface area of the die for the different interconnections. Nowadays many dies are connected to their package by an array of small solder balls underneath the die, called flip chip .

===== [p120-120] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.2.3.1 Camera resolution (1块) =====
--[p120]-- D.4.2.3.1 Camera resolution
The glass plate markers are measured with the SC, which has limited accuracy due to a limited number of pixels. The pixel size, obtained by dividing the field of view over the amount of pixels, is about 5 um. By using algorithms that asses for example the grey levels around the detected edge, a sub pixel resolution can be obtained. The resolution/pixel size ratio is estimated at a factor of 10. This results in a camera accuracy of about 0.5 um.
Some general information about sub‐pixel resolution can be found on the following websites [38], [39].

--[p119]-- D.4.1.3.2 Prepositioning(measurementofsubstratemarkerwithSC)
The SC moves to and measures a reference marker on the substrate and measures its location. From this the offline stroke to the placement location is calculated.

===== [p197-197] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > K.4.2 Electrostatic force (2块) =====
--[p197]-- \begin{array} { r l } & { E _ { { e q \_ J } R } = \cfrac { C _ { a i r } \cdot V _ { a i r } ^ { \textit { \texttt { 2 } } } } { 2 } = \cfrac { A \cdot \varepsilon _ { 0 } \cdot V _ { a i r } ^ { 2 } } { 2 d _ { a i r } } } \\ & { f _ { { e q \_ J } R } = \cfrac { F _ { e q } } { A } = \cfrac { 1 } { A } \cdot \cfrac { \partial E _ { e q } } { \partial d _ { e q } } = - \cfrac { 1 } { 2 } \varepsilon _ { 0 } \cfrac { V _ { a i r } ^ { 2 } } { d _ { a i r } ^ { 2 } } } \end{array}
$$
--[p197]-- K.4.2 Electrostatic force
The pressure to be obtained in a JR clamp is quickly an order higher than what can be obtained in a purely capacitive clamp [51] [52]. The reason is the much smaller distance between the two charged electrodes. Since charge has gathered on the top of the insulator, the effective distance is now only the air gap.
$$
C _ { a i r } = \varepsilon _ { 0 } \frac { A } { d _ { a i r } }
$$
Resulting in the following stored energy and electrostatic pressure.
$$

===== [p61-61] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 5.5 Conclusion (1块) =====
--[p61]-- 5.5 Conclusion
Many of the existing Datacon machine components can be used for the new concept. It is also likely that current state‐of‐the‐art equipment or newly developed technology is adequate to meet the accuracy requirement under the assumption that the vibrations resulting from reaction forces are mitigated. The system that is currently not commercially available is the flexible carrier system itself.
The conceptual design and future design constraints of this system are discussed in the following chapters.

===== [p210-210] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.2.2 Accelerations (OPTIONAL) (1块) =====
--[p210]-- L.2.2 Accelerations (OPTIONAL)
By accelerating the clamp and sample perpendicular to the holding force, the friction between sample and clamp can be investigated. Keeping the clamp at a constant voltage, the whole is moved back and forth. Two effects can be characterized here.
• Effect of sample inertia and friction on the required holding force (constant acceleration)
 Effect of air resistance of the sample on the required holding force (constant speed)
Note: This experiment was not performed since the linear motor (Gantry in Datacon machine) was unavailable at the time.

===== [p72-72] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > Chapter 7 Development of electrostatic clamp (1块) =====
--[p72]-- Chapter 7 Development of electrostatic clamp
To be able to design a good electrostatic carrier, one should get a good idea of the boundary conditions acting on such a system. To accomplish this, a further analysis of the limitations of the clamp was performed and a test setup was designed. Finally measurements were performed on the holding force of a simple clamp.

===== [p116-116] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.1.2 Auto calibration (1块) =====
--[p116]-- D.4.1.2 Auto calibration
The auto calibration routine is performed cyclic, in contrary to the base calibration which is only performed once. The auto calibration is an automatic measurement routine which calibrates all assembly parts and matches both gantry beams to one coordinate system called G0 [35]. In the calibration routine several sub routines can be distinguished.
 Substrate camera calibration / focus length
 G0 matching of left and right gantry
 Bond head  SC offset
 Uplooking camera (ULC) center
 ULC calibration
 FOCT measurement (thermal drift compensation uplooking camera)
BMC test
 Bond force calibration

===== [p201-201] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > K.7.1 Clamping force comparison (2块) =====
--[p201]-- K.7.1 Clamping force comparison
The difference between the monopolar and bipolar or multipolar clamp is that the monopolar clamp only has one electrode pair, while the bipolar and multipolar clamps have multiple. There is a misunderstanding about the clamping force that can be obtained with a bipolar clamp as compared to a monopolar one. Sometimes it is reported that the holding force of a bipolar clamp is much less than that of a monopolar one. This is however incorrect. Interpreting Fig.
--[p201]-- 9‐112; the right picture shows the bipolar clamp on which both a positive and negative voltage are applied. Due to the charge redistribution inside the clamped object, the middle of the clamped object is at ground potential. For determining the clamping force, the electric field in the air gap should be assessed. For the monopolar clamp this is obviously directly related to the single (positive) applied potential.

===== [p124-124] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.2.6 Thermal error (2块) =====
--[p124 [表]]-- This is a measurement which is performed at certain intervals, and not continuously. Therefore the thermal drift in between two measurements is not known. The thermal drift is estimated at about 1 [um] in a half hour. This estimation is based on calculations considering thermal drift of some assembly parts.
Table 9‐6 Estimated thermal drift of assembly parts
Part | Length (mm) | CTE (1/K) | ∆T (K) | ∆I (μm)
Gantry | 50 | $2 3 ^ { * } { 1 0 } ^ { - 6 }$ | 5 | 2.9
Bond head | 20 | $2 3 ^ { * } { 1 0 } ^ { - 6 }$ | 5 | 2.3
Substrate | 10 | $1 7 ^ { * } { 1 0 } ^ { - 6 }$ | 5 | 0.85
--[p124]-- D.4.2.6 Thermal error
A temperature change in the machine can occur either because of fluctuations of the ambient air temperature or by heat generation inside the machine (dissipation of actuators and electronics). These temperature fluctuations cause the machine to expand and contract. This affects the placement accuracy. The FOCT calibration measures the thermal drift of two markers attached to the frame near the ULC’s.

===== [p120-120] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.2.2 Gantry scales (1块) =====
--[p120]-- D.4.2.2 Gantry scales
Since the glass plate is matched to the gantry system, any error in the gantry measurement system (scales) results in a calibration error. The pitch of the scales is 20 μm. Interpolation of the measured data with a factor 100, results in an effective resolution of about 0.2 μm. Earlier measurements have shown that the machine is actually capable of performing 200 nm steps, at least for the x‐direction [37].
$$
u _ { r e s } = \frac { u _ { p i t c h } } { I n t e r p o l a t i o n } = \frac { 2 0 [ \mu m ] } { 1 0 0 } = 2 0 0 [ n m ]
$$

===== [p197-197] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > K.4.1.1 Simplified network (1块) =====
--[p197]-- K.4.1.1 Simplified network
The electric network can be simplified to simplify analysis of the JR clamp. Since the voltage distribution is assumed to be caused by the resistances rather than the capacitors, the capacitance over the insulation layer can be neglected. Also, because the resistance of the insulator is much greater than the wire resistance, these wire resistance can be neglected as well.

===== [p188-188] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > Appendix K Electrostatics (1块) =====
--[p188]-- Appendix K Electrostatics

===== [p223-223] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.8.2.1 Peeling (2块) =====
--[p223]-- L.8.2.1 Peeling
For many measurements, the registered force was less than expected. The peeling effect is most likely the cause of this. If the pulling force is not supplied in the middle of the sample, the sample will tilt (Fig. 9‐147). This increases the air gap on one side of the sample. Thereby reducing the holding force here. Since now already part of the holding force is lost, the maximum force registered by the scale is now lower than the actual clamping force.
--[p223]-- The peeling effect is also a potential problem for the electrostatic flexible carrier. When routed along bends (Fig. 9‐87), the carrier could deform more than the die, thereby increasing the air gap again. This problem should be addressed in the carrier and carrier transport system design. It is recommended that the carrier itself is stiffer than the die, to avoid it bending too much, at least to below an acceptable level so the holding pressure is sufficient to avoid peeling.
It is even better to have the section of the carrier attached to the die stiff enough to avoid bending at all.

===== [p57-57] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 5.2.2 Inspection (1块) =====
--[p57]-- 5.2.2 Inspection
Before and/or after cleaning it is important to have an inspection step. This allows to check for dies that are mechanically damaged, not sufficiently cleaned or defective otherwise. The shortcomings of these dies can then be fixed, or the dies can be disposed, avoiding the assembly of a faulty die and thereby avoiding the additional cost of disposing the good dies in its stack.

===== [p62-62] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > Chapter 6 Dies on flexible carrier (1块) =====
--[p62]-- Chapter 6 Dies on flexible carrier
In Chapter 4 the flexible carrier concept was proposed as new PnP concept for 3D‐IC stacking. Many components of this system, like for example stages, camera’s and other measurement systems, are available off‐the‐shelf. However, the flexible carrier system itself is not. This makes this an interesting subject for further research.

===== [p63-63] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 6.3 Clamping mechanisms (1块) =====
--[p63]-- 6.3 Clamping mechanisms
Several clamping mechanisms were investigated for the current task of providing a temporary bond between die and carrier (Table 6‐2). This section summarizes the viable concepts and their advantages and disadvantages. To get a more thorough understanding of all proposed mechanisms it is recommended to read Appendix J in addition to this section. The three most likely systems are discussed in this section.

===== [p110-110] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.1.1.4 Phi­Z actuator (1块) =====
--[p110]-- D.1.1.4 Phi­Z actuator
The Phi‐Z module is attached to the Y‐stroke of the gantry (Fig. 9‐20). The nozzle which picks and places the dies is directly attached to this module. The actuators provide the gantry system with the ability to move in vertical direction and rotate the die about the vertical axis.

===== [p34-34] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 3.2.1 Workings of the Datacon machine (1块) =====
--[p34]-- 3.2.1 Workings of the Datacon machine
The used process to pick dies from a wafer and place them on a substrate is as follows. The full functional diagram is included in app. D.3.

===== [p172-172] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > J.2.1 Considerable incompatibilities clamping methods and thin bare die (4块) =====
--[p172]-- J.2.1 Considerable incompatibilities clamping methods and thin bare die
Some of the above mentioned concepts are not viable for the following reasons. First, holding a die with magnetism requires a metal inside the die which supports it. It is not desirable to limit the usability of this carrier only to dies which have certain magnetic properties.
In the current definition, friction relies on the interlocking of voids and pores of two surfaces<sup>15</sup>.
--[p172]-- It is not desirable to have restrictions on the surface requirements of the die for a temporary bond. Also, if no external mechanism is used, the total friction which can be obtained by using gravity limits the accelerations which can be used.
$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { F _ { f r i c t i o n } = m \cdot g \cdot \mu _ { C O F } } \\ & { F _ { a c c e l e r a t i o n } = m \cdot a } \end{array} \bigg \} m \cdot g \cdot \mu _ { C O F } \geq m \cdot a \Rightarrow a \leq g \cdot \mu _ { C O F }
$$
--[p172]-- The die‐tape connection should be able to handle accelerations in the order of $2 5 [ \mathsf { m } / \mathsf { s } ^ { 2 } ]$ . This would result in a required COF of about 2.5, which is not realistic. Also, when relying on gravity, the carrier cannot be used upside down or vertical, which limits the ways in which the total system can be configured. Third, mechanical clamping is also not desirable.
--[p172]-- It increases the risk of damaging the die during transport because, with the added risk of contamination. Also, the end effectors themselves are a potential source of contamination. Mechanical clamping is usually not a simple process and requires at least one moving actuator per die, which increases the cost of the carrier significantly.

===== [p78-78] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 7.2.2 Electrode shape (3块) =====
--[p78]-- 7‐15, the right most design was chosen for the application of wafer clamping during plasma processing. The reason was the homogenous electric field beneath most of the wafer. For the current application no obvious benefits were evident for this design. Even more, this design restricts the die sizes that can be used. Because of the simplicity of the two D‐shaped electrodes, this design was chosen.
The D‐shaped electrodes can be chosen slightly larger or smaller than the object to be clamped.
--[p78]-- 7.2.2 Electrode shape
For the reasons discussed in Chapter 6, a bipolar or multipolar clamp was chosen. To keep the test setup and electrode design simple, a bipolar clamp will be used for the experiments. As described in [23], there are several bipolar and multipolar electrode designs industrially available.
The simplest, and therefore the easiest and cheapest to manufacture, of these designs are the two D‐shaped electrodes. In the paper referred to in Fig.
--[p78]-- Using a smaller clamp results in less holding force given that the electrostatic force is proportional to the clamp area. If larger electrodes are used, some of the clamp area is not being covered by the die/wafer. This area causes electrostatic fringe fields that attract dust to the clamp. For the force measurements it is beneficial to have a large force as possible. For that reason it was chosen to make the electrodes slightly larger than the clamped object.
This also creates the possibility of assessing how severe the effect of attracting dust really is.

===== [p114-114] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.2 Current alignment process (2块) =====
--[p114]-- D.2 Current alignment process
Measurements resulting in the final alignment accuracy are performed in three steps.
First a base calibration is performed to compensate for mechanical inaccuracies of the XY systems. Reference is the Substrate Camera (SC in bondhead). This calibration in only performed once. More information is found in the machine manual [34]
--[p114]--  Auto calibration is performed periodically to match the coordinate system of the XY system and other assembly parts to the global machine coordinate system. More information is found in [34]
 Alignment during machine operation (pick and place process), which is illustrated in Fig. 9‐26.
1. Measure die location on nozzle
2. Measure bondhead location above to substrate (fiducials)
3. Make final placement step (distance SC to nozzle + found position error)

===== [p196-197] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > K.4.1 Electrical network (3块) =====
--[p196]-- K.4.1 Electrical network
The electrical network was discussed in app. K.3.1. For convenience, the picture is shown below once more.
The difference with the discussion in app. K.3.1 is the resistance over the capacitors, which is not assumed infinite here. After the capacitors are charged, a small current runs through the resistances. This allows charge redistribution. The voltage divider is now not determined by the ratio of the capacitances but by the ratio of the resistances.
$$
--[p197]-- The voltage over the air layer builds up over time, the rate of build up is related to the insulator resistivity (discussed later). If the resistance on the insulator‐clamped object interface is of the same order as the resistance of the dielectric material, the voltage over the interface is a significant part of the applied voltage.
--[p197]-- \begin{array} { r } { V _ { a i r } = V _ { s } \cdot \cfrac { Z _ { a i r } } { Z _ { a i r } + Z _ { i n s } } \approx V _ { s } \cdot \cfrac { R _ { a i r } } { R _ { a i r } + R _ { i n s } } } \\ { V _ { i n s } = V _ { s } \cdot \cfrac { Z _ { i n s } } { Z _ { a i r } + Z _ { i n s } } \approx V _ { s } \cdot \cfrac { R _ { i n s } } { R _ { a i r } + R _ { i n s } } } \end{array}
$$

===== [p43-43] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 4.1.1 Serial and parallel processes (5块) =====
--[p43 [表]]-- 4.1.1 Serial and parallel processes
When looking at a single die the first four actions discussed earlier have to be performed in series. The other two can be placed either in series or parallel to these actions. The possible operation sequences of serial and parallel processes for a single die are summarized in (Table 4‐1):
Table 4‐1 Possible sequences of operations (S1 – S12), depending on using parallel processes
Legend | Sequence
S1 | S2 | S3 | S4 | S5 | S6 | S7 | S8 | S9 | S10 S11 | S12
--[p43]-- Release die from source | A A | A | A | A | A | A | A | A | A A | A | A
Pick die from source | B B | B | B | B | B,F | B,F | B | B B | B | B,F | B
Transport die to target | C F | C | M | C | C,M | C | C,F F | F | C | C | C,F
Place die on target | D C | F | F | M | D | D,M | D,M | C,M C | F | M | M
Flip die | F M | M | C | F | D | D,M D,M | D | D
Measurements | M D | D | D | D
 Perform different actions on different dies simultaneous
 Perform the same action on multiple dies simultaneous
--[p43]-- Basically all actions can be performed parallel when performing different actions on different dies simultaneous. However, when looking at performing the same action on multiple dies, there are limits. It is for example possible to use many individually actuated grippers. Moving this array of grippers through the process steps causes parallelism.
--[p43]-- Therefore, creating parallelism by performing the same action on multiple dies should only be considered in case an action is a serious bottleneck in the cycle time and it is not possible to use multiple separate machines for this task. This discussion about parallel operations resulted in the development of several preliminary concepts.
--[p43]-- This is however a very expensive solution since the amount of required actuators is equal to the amount needed for a full serial process multiplied with the amount of dies handled at once. Therefore this has many similarities to just using multiple machines, which is actually better for process reliability in case of a failure of one machine.

===== [p82-82] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 7.3.3 Force measurements (2块) =====
--[p82]-- 7.3.3 Force measurements
Three series of force measurements were performed, the analysis of which is discussed in app. L.5. Here a summary is given of the final results of the third series.
Fig. 7‐24 shows the pressure changing with applied voltage. To put this in perspective, for a 4.6 [μm] air gap the actual potential over the air gap is about 12% of this. The voltage difference over the die is twice the air gap potential for a bipolar clamp.
--[p82]-- In the graph, the solid green line shows the expected pressure based on the measured capacitance. This line was created with the Matlab scripts written for the analysis of the electrostatic clamp (Appendix K). The dashed green lines show the possible variance based on air gap height uncertainty and specified force sensor non‐linearity causing measurement uncertainty (datasheet sensor see Fig. 9‐121).

===== [p64-64] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 6.3.1 Chemical adhesive (2块) =====
--[p64]-- 6.3.1 Chemical adhesive
The most basic way (and a very commonly used way as discussed above) to attach electronic components to a flexible carrier is by using an adhesive. Chemical adhesives are used extensively throughout the different semiconductor processes. Examples are for instance, dicing tapes that hold bare dies in configuration after separation and tape and reel systems that are used in component placers.
--[p64]-- Despite the obvious solution, there are some considerable downsides in using adhesive tapes for thin bare dies. The advantages and disadvantages of adhesives are summarized below.

===== [p170-170] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > J.1.3 Operation related (1块) =====
--[p170]-- J.1.3 Operation related
For the flexible carrier concept to reach its full potential in being a flexible easily routable carrier, some values are assumed for the routing of the carrier. These values are not ‘hard’ requirements but make it easier to put the design of the flexible carrier system into a perspective.

===== [p182-182] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > J.3.1 Considered forces (2块) =====
--[p182]-- J.3.1 Considered forces
During displacement of the clamped die, several forces are acting on the die which should be resisted by the clamping force. The most important forces are forces due to:
 Accelerations including gravity
Forces due to interactions with the air
Bending of the die
Bending forces occur when the clamp base material is not stiff enough and deforms when going around bends. This results in turn in bending of the die or release of the die if the clamping force is not sufficient.
--[p182]-- Besides the forces mentioned above there are also other forces acting on the die like chemical adhesion to the tape and van der Waals forces. These forces, and other, forces are neglected here since the magnitude of these forces is much less. Fig. 9‐86 and Fig. 9‐87 illustrate the forces that are discussed in this section.

===== [p153-153] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.8 Contamination (1块) =====
--[p153]-- G.2.8 Contamination
The yield of a concept can be reduced if contamination is added during the PnP process. This criterion concerns the use of contaminating processes and the possibility that the contamination ends up on the wrong surfaces, reducing yield.

===== [p115-115] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.3.3.1 Addition of errors (1块) =====
--[p115]-- D.3.3.1 Addition of errors
Considering the errors are determined by a stochastic process the errors (standard deviation) can be added quadratic[36]
$$
\sigma = \sqrt { a _ { 1 } ^ { 2 } \sigma _ { 1 } ^ { 2 } + a _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } \sigma _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } + a _ { 3 } ^ { 2 } \sigma _ { 3 } ^ { 2 } }
$$

===== [p208-208] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.2 General layout of test setup (2块) =====
--[p208]-- First, there is the lower bipolar electrostatic electrode on which a voltage is supplied to create the attractive force. Second, there is the upper electrode. This is a dummy for the actual die and can be either made of silicon or from another conductive material. The design of both are discussed in section L.3. First, the test setup is discussed.
--[p208]-- L.2 General layout of test setup
The aim of this test setup is to get a general idea of the holding force that can be obtained. Also, it is useful to see how the clamp will react to accelerations. However, this experiment is optional because of the limited time available. The targeted holding pressure was about 100 Pa, which translates to about 10 [mN] for a 10x10 [mm<sup>2</sup>] clamp.
For all test setups two parts are required.

===== [p35-35] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 3.2.2.1 Throughput (3块) =====
--[p35 [表]]-- Also, increased movement speeds result in higher vibration levels, affecting accuracy.
Table 3‐1 Used time for different process steps during PnP cycle
Time [ms] Share in cycle time
Gantry movement | 1200
75% 80 5%
Settling time Measurements | 240 15%
Other operations (e.g. picking, placing) | 140 10%
--[p35]-- It was obvious that the gantry system (XY positioning) was always busy, and most of the time moving, and therefore the limiting factor in terms of throughput. Table 3‐1 shows an estimation of the time used for some different machine operations on the critical path. An increase in throughput would require an increase in the operation speeds of the gantry. As will be discussed in the next chapter, this would be a very difficult goal to reach.
--[p35]-- 3.2.2.1 Throughput
The throughput of the current machine is in the order of 3600 to 8000 components per hour, depending on the type of component assembled and the required placement accuracy. For the high accuracy mode (10 [μm] 3σ), the machine timings were investigated (see app. D.3) to quantify the throughput limitations of the current machine.

===== [p213-213] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.4.2 Holding force measurements (1块) =====
--[p213]-- L.4.2 Holding force measurements
Tests were carried out in clean bench to minimize contamination of clamping surfaces. The electrodes (clamp and sample) were cleaned with alcohol between measurements to remove any charge that has build up at the interface.

===== [p168-168] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > J.1 Requirements (1块) =====
--[p168]-- J.1 Requirements

===== [p57-57] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 5.2.1 Cleaning (2块) =====
--[p57]-- 5.2.1 Cleaning
Before PnP, the exposed side of the source wafer was cleaned. The other side of the die, the one attached to the dicing tape, is not yet cleaned. Based on current research performed within the BlueBird project, It is very likely that a cleaning step is required between pick and place to remove any particles and contamination from this side of the die. Possible sources of contamination are.
 Particles in the dicing street that were not removed during earlier cleaning
--[p57]-- Residue from the dicing tape adhesive.
Since the side of the die earlier attached to the dicing tape is accessible when the die is mounted on the flexible carrier, this is a good opportunity to clean it.

===== [p32-32] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment (1块) =====
--[p32]-- Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment
The first section describes the possibilities and applications of current pick and place equipment and their limitations in the scope of 3D‐IC. In the next section a more thorough analysis is performed on a single pick and place machine, the Datacon 8800 FC Quantum. Finally more general performance limitations are discussed which result from the basic principle of operation of current pick and place equipment.

===== [p116-116] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.1.1 Base calibration (1块) =====
--[p116]-- D.4.1.1 Base calibration
A glass plate with a grid of markers is placed in the machine. The Substrate Camera (SC) is moved over the markers and the gantry scales are read out at each marker. This results in $1 ^ { \mathfrak { s t } }$ and 2+ order deviations in gantry movement with respect to the ‘true axis’ represented by the glass plate. The errors which occur in the base calibration routine are described below.

===== [p188-188] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > K.1 Introduction (2块) =====
--[p188]-- K.1 Introduction
In this document the background of electrostatic clamping of bare dies is discussed. Aspects important to electrostatic clamp design are the clamping force or pressure and clamping and declamping times. These aspects can be affected by the choice of dielectric material, air gap height and leak currents. Also, the risk of damage  to the electronic circuits due to the applied electric field is discussed.
There are two categories into which electrostatic clamps can be divided.
--[p188]-- First, there is the capacitive clamp, which is similar to a capacitor, two conductive plates with a dielectric material between them. The electric field is build up by directly applying a voltage difference to the electrodes or by charge distribution between capacitors in series connection. Second, there is the effect of Johnson Rahbek forces. These forces are build up on a much longer time scale by charge build up by the leakage currents through the ohmic electric resistances in capacitors.
Matlab and 20‐Sim models were created for the graphs in this section. These are supplied on the complementary DVD.

===== [p37-37] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 3.3 Current speed limitations (2块) =====
--[p37]-- 3.3 Current speed limitations
In current pick and place equipment 60% – 80% of the cycle time is used to move the dies, while about 40% ‐ 20% of the time is used to actually measure or place dies (see also Table 3‐1). Typical performance of current state‐of‐the‐art machines is 5 dies/s at 50 [μm] accuracy or 2.5 [μm] accuracy at 0.4 dies/s (different machine). Most of these machines use a gantry system, to which one or more vacuum nozzles are attached for part manipulation.
--[p37]-- These gantries have to move back and forth between the source and target location. To find the throughput limits of this design, an analysis was performed based on motion profiles. The more extensive presentation form of this section is included in Appendix E.

===== [p48-49] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 4.4.1 Turret concept (2块) =====
--[p48]-- 4.4.1 Turret concept
Dies are picked from the source wafer either by the turret itself or by an optional flip unit. During the rotation of the turret, additional (parallel) process steps can be performed to the dies which are cleared from the pick and place locations. When a die reaches the placement location, a short stroke z‐φ actuator places the die on the target wafer. The XY positioning is performed by the wafer stage. Optionally, the rotation DOF can be located at the wafer stage as well.
--[p49]-- The position measurement required for final alignment can be implemented in two ways.
 Relative measurement of target and die at the placement location
 First measure die relative to nozzle (arbitrary location on turret). At the placement location only the nozzle relative to the wafer is measured.

===== [p220-220] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.6 Failing of $\mathbf { S i O _ { 2 } }$ insulated clamp (1块) =====
--[p220]-- L.6 Failing of $\mathbf { S i O _ { 2 } }$ insulated clamp
As stated in section L.3.1, two different clamps were manufactured. One with a Kapton HN foil as insulator of which the measurement results were discussed above. The other one used an $\mathsf { S i O } _ { 2 }$ layer as insulator, which was created using CVD.

===== [p221-221] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.6.1 Breakdown of clamp (3块) =====
--[p221]-- L.6.1 Breakdown of clamp
Expecting a dielectric breakdown, the voltage over the clamp was increased very slowly during the experiments, starting at 0 [V]. At several volts, no force was measured. When the voltage was increased a bit more the clamp failed by dielectric breakdown. The clamp failed while increasing the voltage from 5 to 10 [V]. The exact value is not known since the voltage source was still building up potential while the clamp failed.
--[p221]-- The failing of the clamp was noticed by the volt meters on the voltage sources dropping to zero volt, which indicated a short circuit. After disconnecting the power and removing the electrode the damage was obvious. Some pictures of the destroyed clamp and damaged sample electrode are shown in (Fig. 9‐142 to Fig. 9‐145)
--[p221]-- The insulator on both electrodes of the bipolar clamp was destroyed, explaining the short circuit since the sample electrode now provided the electrical connection between both electrodes of the clamp. At the location where the breakdown occurred the deposited aluminum electrodes were removed. Pieces of Kapton HN tape (on which the electrodes were deposited) were found embedded in the sample electrode (Fig. 9‐145)

===== [p103-103] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > B.6.4 Innovative (1块) =====
--[p103]-- B.6.4 Innovative
The design must be innovative. This means either a newly developed concept is innovative or adaptations to the existing machine are innovative. Preferably, the design should not be available on the market. The design or part of the design is patentable and the design can be used in follow‐up research.

===== [p170-170] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > J.1.3.2 Position stability (1块) =====
--[p170]-- J.1.3.2 Position stability
The required position and position stability of the die on the carrier is not very strict. It is however desirable to make sure the die in the field of view (FOV) of the camera at the placement location. The camera’s for fiducial detection in the Datacon machine have a FOV of about 5 [mm]. Presenting a die within 10% of the FOV should allow correct detection of the die. Therefore the position stability of the die on the carrier is set at 0.5 [mm].
If the carrier is used as reference for placement this stability requirement is of course more strict (< 1 μm).

===== [p149-149] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > F.10 Wafer to wafer (1块) =====
--[p149]-- F.10 Wafer to wafer
The simplest reservoir or carrier of dies is a diced wafer. If the source and target wafer can be aligned, the transport distance of the dies will be minimal. The total time for component supply is equal to the time needed to align both wafers. Depending on the measurement principle and die release mechanism, very high throughput can be expected. The placement accuracy is however a concern. This concept was subject to research earlier.
The project description is found in [17].
$$
t _ { t o t } = t _ { p r e s }
$$

===== [p153-153] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.7 Possibility separation of high reaction forces and high accuracy (1块) =====
--[p153]-- G.2.7 Possibility separation of high reaction forces and high accuracy
For some concepts it is possible to use separate vibration isolated frames for both the fast moving parts with high reaction forces and the parts where high accuracy is needed. This way the negative effect of the reaction forces is minimized.

===== [p80-80] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 7.2.6 Dummy electrode (2块) =====
--[p80]-- 7.2.6 Dummy electrode
Instead of using thin dies it was decided to use more robust electrodes as dummy for the clamped object. The only requirements on the electrode were that it should be made from conductor or semi‐conductor and a surface roughness and flatness should be about 1 [μm] in order to obtain the correct air gap height. It was decided to use aluminum cubes for this purpose since these were easily manufactured and readily available. Fig.
--[p80]-- 7‐18 shows a photograph of two of these electrodes that were lapped to a semi‐reflective finish.

===== [p212-212] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.3.1.1 Power supply (1块) =====
--[p212]-- L.3.1.1 Power supply
Two 300 [V] Delta voltage sources were used for the experiments. These were connected in series to create both a positive and a negative supply voltage. This way, the clamped object should not build up a net charge relative to ground. A picture of the used voltage sources is shown in Fig. 9‐129. The electrical scheme used to create both the positive and negative voltage is illustrated inFig. 9‐130.

===== [p119-119] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.1.3.3 Movement to place position (1块) =====
--[p119]-- D.4.1.3.3 Movement to place position
The movement to the actual place position is a small movement, the final placement position is however not checked with the SC. Therefore, the only feedback is the readout of the gantry scales. The main errors in this operation are:
Gantry bending
 Gantry scales
 Gantry sensor noise
 Thermal expansion of gantry system
 Thermal expansion of z‐stroke
 Thermal expansion of substrate
 Vibrations

===== [p86-86] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 8.1 Conclusions (1块) =====
--[p86]-- 8.1 Conclusions
To make future 3D‐IC devices a cost effective solution the total cost has to be reduced. One of the main contributions to the cost of a 3D‐IC is the assembly process. To reduce the cost impact of the assembly step, a pick and place machine has to be developed with much increased accuracy and throughput

===== [p111-111] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.1.1.6 Placement location (1块) =====
--[p111]-- D.1.1.6 Placement location
The placement location, as the name implies, is the location where the die is finally placed. The target is transported with a belt system. Empty targets (usually PCB’s, wafers or substrates) enter the machine on the left, are held in place by a vacuum system at the placement location, and exit the machine on the right.

===== [p153-153] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.3 Possibility for continuous process (1块) =====
--[p153]-- G.2.3 Possibility for continuous process
The estimated UPH was calculated using the principle that components are picked up and placed while stationary. This creates lots of start stop operations $( 3 ^ { \mathsf { r d } }$ order setpoints) in which a lot of time is lost accelerating and decelerating. This criteria concerns the possibility that a concept can be implemented with continuous operation (increasing throughput).

===== [p58-58] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 5.3 Alignment (1块) =====
--[p58]-- 5.3 Alignment
As stated in the requirements (chapter Chapter 2), it is preferred to use the by TNO newly developed alignment system. This alignment system and four others, were briefly analyzed in Appendix H. From this analysis it was concluded that of the proposed principles, the newly developed system by TNO is the a feasible concept to reach 1 [μm] 3σ placement accuracy at acceptable cost.

===== [p57-57] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 5.2 Parallel processes (2块) =====
--[p57]-- 5.2 Parallel processes
One of the main strengths of the flexible carrier is that the distance that has to be covered to present the next die is greatly reduced. The other important strength is the possibility to carry out many processes in parallel on different dies. The pick location and place location are decoupled in time, allowing the picking and placing tasks to be performed simultaneously.
--[p57]-- Between the pick location and place location there is a certain length of carrier with dies that is available for other parallel processes. If parallel processes are added, these processes have to fulfill the requirement of processing dies with an average net cycle time of 200 [ms]. This does not mean each process has to be carried out within this timeframe, one can also process dies batch wise, using for example a 600 [ms] process for three dies simultaneous.
Some possible parallel processes and their added value are discussed below.

===== [p207-207] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > K.9 Final notes (2块) =====
--[p207]-- It is however not desirable to have electrodes smaller than the clamped object since this reduces the total holding force. In the ideal case, the electrodes are therefore the same size as the clamped object.
Matlab was used for the generation of some graphs in this appendix. The used mfiles are supplied on the DVD, most of the written code makes use of the symbolic toolbox. Full symbolic models for the capacitive clamp as well as the Johnson Rahbek clamp were also made.
--[p207]-- K.9 Final notes
When designing an electrostatic clamp one should be aware that the formulas used in this chapter represent the case of an ideal parallel plate capacitor. In an actual clamp some fringe fields will exist around the perimeter of the die. These fringe fields could attract contaminating particles. It is therefore desirable to have electrodes that are no larger than the clamped object.

===== [p117-117] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.1.2.4 Uplooking camera center (1块) =====
--[p117]-- D.4.1.2.4 Uplooking camera center
This calibration step determines the position of the ULC center in G0. The uplooking camera center is measured with the bond head and a special tool.

===== [p153-153] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.10 Known good die (1块) =====
--[p153]-- G.2.10 Known good die
If no die or a faulty die is picked up or a die turns out to be faulty after inspection, can the process dispose of this die and present a new die to the placement location with minimal time loss and with minimal damage or contamination to other dies.

===== [p159-159] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > H.3.2.1 Opportunities (1块) =====
--[p159]-- H.3.2.1 Opportunities
This alignment system could be useful if a second flexible carrier is used as assembly location (see section F.8). the markers at the underside of the die will then not be available for alignment. Using markers on the back side of the carrier makes alignment possible again.

===== [p29-29] Department of Precision and Microsystems Engineering > 2.5 Other requirements (1块) =====
--[p29]-- 2.5 Other requirements
To increase the chance of a manufacturer adopting the new concept, it is best to keep the new machine layout close to the current one. Also, since the pick and place machine is back‐end semiconductor equipment, the cost of the machine is certainly an issue. For this reason it is preferred not to use ‘exotic’ materials, costly bearings or very expensive actuators and sensors, which are common in front‐end semiconductor equipment.

===== [p153-153] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.9 Inspection (1块) =====
--[p153]-- G.2.9 Inspection
If a contaminated or faulty die enters the PnP cycle or contamination is added during the PnP cycle, an inspection step can detect this. For some processes it is possible to implement an inspection step before placement, reducing the number of faulty dies placed, increasing yield. Preferably the inspection step is implemented parallel to an existing step to minimize the effect on throughput.

===== [p222-222] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.7 Other performed tests (1块) =====
--[p222]-- L.7 Other performed tests
Early on in designing the test setup it was decided to use aluminum sample electrodes instead of (thin) dies or wafers. In addition to these force measurements, it was verified that the clamp can in fact pick up dies and wafers. The pictures below illustrate this. Movies of the pickup and release are available on the supplied DVD.

===== [p39-39] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 3.3.2 Conclusions and discussion (2块) =====
--[p39]-- 3.3.2 Conclusions and discussion
A single robot with a single nozzle is not a realistic option for achieving a <200 [ms] cycle time over a large travel distance of 300 [mm]. To increase throughput and reduce the effective cycle time the travel distance needs to be reduced or some degree of parallelism needs to be introduced. To increase parallelism one can either actuate in the horizontal plane and vertical direction simultaneous or use multiple parallel robots.
--[p39]-- Another solution to reduce the travel time is to avoid starting and stopping since there is a lot of time lost in these events. For example, a 300 [mm] distance is covered in 100 [ms] with a constant velocity of 3 [m/s], which is not an extreme value. Making use of parallel actions and transporting dies at constant velocity are the main input for the design of preliminary concepts as discussed in the next chapter.

===== [p153-153] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.6 Possibility of direct relative measurement die – target (1块) =====
--[p153]-- G.2.6 Possibility of direct relative measurement die – target
A direct relative measurement of die to target often results in higher accuracy than a combination of two measurements resulting in a relative measurement. The reason for this is that the measurement errors are stacked and add up to a larger error than that of a single direct relative measurement. The effect of error propagation was discussed in appendix D.4.

===== [p80-80] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > 7.3.1 Overview of test setup (1块) =====
--[p80]-- 7.3.1 Overview of test setup
To evaluate the holding force of the electrostatic clamp a simple test setup was made. The schematic overview of this setup is shown in Fig. 7‐20 and photos of the actual setup are shown in Fig. 7‐21 to Fig. 7‐23. Note that two voltage sources were used to supply both a positive and negative voltage to the clamp. The discussion below only names the positive voltages, but note that a negative voltage of the same magnitude was always used complementary.

===== [p209-209] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.2.1.2 Scale (1块) =====
--[p209]-- L.2.1.2 Scale
The used scale is the ‘Advanced force gauge 2.5 N’. The full range of this gauge is ±2.5 [N] with a 0.5 [mN] resolution (see Fig. 9‐121). This range is sufficient for the targeted holding forces which are in the order of 10 [mN] (100 [Pa] over 1 [cm<sup>2</sup>] area). The sensor has an accuracy of 0.1% full‐scale, which translates to 2.5 [mN]. During the measurements ‘max hold’ was used to obtain the maximum pulling force of the sample.

===== [p141-141] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > F.5 Turret head (1块) =====
--[p141]-- F.5 Turret head
Instead of placing the turret head on a gantry one can use a separate system to load the turret head. And use the radius of the turret itself to bridge the gap between the source and target location. This way a continuous supply of dies is available for the placement head(s) and the cycle time is reduced. Another benefit is that the system which needs high accuracy can be placed on a separate frame from the low accuracy, high speed supply system.

===== [p154-154] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > G.2.14 Risk new process (1块) =====
--[p154]-- G.2.14 Risk new process
The use of a new, unproven, technology is a risk. There are several concepts which have proven successful in handling dies and placing them at high accuracy. Deviating from the current solutions is a risk in the way that a developed concept could potentially not work or reduce the interest of the back‐end industry in the design.

===== [p120-120] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.4.1.3.4 Placement (1块) =====
--[p120]-- D.4.1.3.4 Placement
The placement is the downward motion of the nozzle, with which the die is finally placed on the substrate. During the downward motion of the nozzle there is no horizontal position change to correct for placement errors.
 Orientation of die in Rx,Ry
Orientation of substrate in Rx,Ry
 Release from nozzle
 Sensor accuracy zposition
 Straightness of placement motion

===== [p142-142] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > F.5.1 Nozzles radial (1块) =====
--[p142]-- F.5.1 Nozzles radial
The source wafer is placed on a wafer stage. The wafer stage positions a die under the pickup position of the flip unit. The flip unit presents the die to the turret head. The die can be picked up during placement of another die on the target wafer or during rotation of the turret.

===== [p117-117] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > Measured value: uplooking camera center in G0 (1块) =====
--[p117]-- Measured value: uplooking camera center in G0
The ULC calibration tool is moved to the ULC, an iterative process places the ULC calibration tool in the center of the ULC by evaluating its image. The ULC position in G0 is calculated by using the bond head  substrate camera offset and measuring the SC position with the gantry scales. Contributing error sources are:
Bond head  substrate camera offset error
 G0 matching
 Gantry scales
 Theta accuracy
 ULC accuracy
 ULC lens
 ULC tool accuracy
 Vibrations (external, roller guide, reaction forces)

===== [p115-115] Chapter 3 Analysis current pick and place equipment > D.3.3 Error tree (1块) =====
--[p115]-- D.3.3 Error tree
The final placement accuracy as stated by Datacon is 10 [μm] (3σ). In an attempt to find the performance limiting processes in terms of placement error, an error tree was built. The error tree takes into account the errors caused by the base calibration, auto calibration and the pick and place cycle.
The error tree is constructed based on calculations as described in chapter D.4, as well as on the performed vibration measurements Appendix M.
The error tree itself is included in Table 9‐9 to Table 9‐11.

===== [p213-213] Appendix L Experimental investigation of electrostatic tape > L.4.2.2 Measurement (1块) =====
--[p213]-- L.4.2.2 Measurement
 Release tension from spring
 Make sure surfaces of electrodes are clean
 Apply constant voltage ranging from ±40 V to ±250 V is applied to the clamp.
Reset force sensor
 Place top electrode on bottom electrode
 Pull electrode off
 Record min and max force from force sensor
 Release voltage from clamp or repeat measurement
